11. using System;
12. using System.Collections.Generic;
13. using Windows.ApplicationModel.Background;
14. using Windows.Data.Json;
15. using Windows.Devices.Geolocation;
16. using Windows.Globalization;
17. using Windows.Globalization.DateTimeFormatting;
18. using Windows.Storage;
19. 
20. namespace BackgroundTask
21. {
22.     // TODO[migrate-020]: see PATTERNS.md#background-tasks
23.     public sealed class VisitBackgroundTask : IBackgroundTask
24.     {
25.         // Value determined by how many max length event descriptors (91 chars)
26.         // stored as a JSON string can fit in 8K (max allowed for local settings)
27.         private const int maxEventDescriptors = 42;
28. 
29.         // LocalSettings persistent data.
30.         IDictionary<string, object> _values = ApplicationData.Current.LocalSettings.Values;
31. 
32.         // TODO[migrate-021]: see PATTERNS.md#background-tasks
33.         void IBackgroundTask.Run(IBackgroundTaskInstance taskInstance)
34.         {
35.             BackgroundTaskDeferral deferral = taskInstance.GetDeferral();
36.             GeovisitTriggerDetails triggerDetails = (GeovisitTriggerDetails)taskInstance.TriggerDetails;
37. 
38.             // Handle Visit reports
39.             ProcessVisitReports(triggerDetails);
40.             deferral.Complete();
41.         }
42. 
43.         private void ProcessVisitReports(GeovisitTriggerDetails triggerDetails)
44.         {
45.             // Load the values saved from last time.
46.             JsonArray visitBackgroundEvents = Helpers.LoadSavedJson("BackgroundVisitEventCollection");
47. 
48.             // Read reports from the triggerDetails.
49.             IReadOnlyList<Geovisit> reports = triggerDetails.ReadReports();
50. 
51.             string description = null;
52. 
53.             foreach (Geovisit report in reports)
54.             {
55.                 description = GenerateVisitDescription(report);
56. 
57.                 // Add to the collection, trimming old events if necessary.
58.                 if (visitBackgroundEvents.Count == maxEventDescriptors)
59.                 {
60.                     visitBackgroundEvents.RemoveAt(maxEventDescriptors - 1);
61.                 }
62.                 visitBackgroundEvents.Insert(0, JsonValue.CreateStringValue(description));
63.             }
64. 
65.             int numReports = reports.Count;
66.             if (numReports > 0)
67.             {
68.                 // Save the modified results for next time.
69.                 _values["BackgroundVisitEventCollection"] = visitBackgroundEvents.Stringify();
70. 
71.                 if (numReports > 1)
72.                 {
73.                     description = $"There are {numReports} new geofence events";
74.                 }
75.                 Helpers.DisplayToast(description);
76.             }
77.         }
78. 
79.         private string GenerateVisitDescription(Geovisit visit)
80.         {
81.             string result = $"{visit.Timestamp:G} {visit.StateChange}";
82. 
83.             // Check if the report has a valid position.
84.             if (visit.Position != null)
85.             {
86.                 result += $" ({visit.Position.Coordinate.Point.Position.Latitude}, {visit.Position.Coordinate.Point.Position.Longitude})";
87.             }
88. 
89.             return result;
90.         }
91.     }
92. }

<a id="threading"></a>
## Threading: CoreDispatcher → DispatcherQueue

UWP (replace this):

```csharp
await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () => StatusText.Text = "Done");
```

WinUI 3:

```csharp
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(() => StatusText.Text = "Done");
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(DispatcherQueuePriority.High, () => ProgressBar.Value = 100);
```

Cache the queue off the UI thread via `DispatcherQueue.GetForCurrentThread()`. UWP's ASTA reentrancy protection is gone — watch for reentrancy in async code that pumps messages. See the official [threading guide](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/threading).
<a id="windowing"></a>
## Windowing: Window.Current / ApplicationView / CoreWindow → AppWindow

`Window.Current`, `ApplicationView`, and `CoreWindow` are gone. Track the main window yourself and use `Microsoft.UI.Windowing.AppWindow`. See the official [windowing guide](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/windowing).

UWP (replace this):

```csharp
var win = Window.Current;
ApplicationView.GetForCurrentView().TryResizeView(new Size(800, 600));
```

WinUI 3 — expose `MainWindow` from `App`:

```csharp
public partial class App : Application
{
    // With <Nullable>enable</Nullable>, declare as nullable since the assignment only happens during OnLaunched.
    public static Window? MainWindow { get; private set; }

    protected override void OnLaunched(LaunchActivatedEventArgs args)
    {
        var window = new MainWindow();
        MainWindow = window;
        window.Activate();
    }
}
```

Consumers running after `OnLaunched` can safely null-forgive:

```csharp
var hwnd = WinRT.Interop.WindowNative.GetWindowHandle(App.MainWindow!);
```

Resize via `AppWindow`:

```csharp
var hwnd = WinRT.Interop.WindowNative.GetWindowHandle(App.MainWindow);
var windowId = Microsoft.UI.Win32Interop.GetWindowIdFromWindow(hwnd);
var appWindow = AppWindow.GetFromWindowId(windowId);
appWindow.Resize(new SizeInt32(800, 600));
```

### Initialization order — keep MainWindow's constructor inert

`App.MainWindow` (or any equivalent static window reference) is `null` for the entire duration of `new MainWindow()` — the right-hand side runs to completion BEFORE the assignment happens. Anything inside that constructor that reads the static — directly or transitively — sees `null` and throws `E_POINTER` (0x80004003) at startup. The build is clean; the analyzer is silent; only a runtime launch reveals it.

APIs that commonly trip this race when called from a `Page` reached during `MainWindow` construction:

- `WinRT.Interop.WindowNative.GetWindowHandle(App.MainWindow)`
- `XamlRoot` / `Content.XamlRoot` access through a static-window helper
- `PrintManagerInterop.GetForWindow(hwnd)` / `ShowPrintUIForWindowAsync(hwnd)`
- `FileOpenPicker` / `FileSavePicker` / `FolderPicker` (require an HWND)
- `ContentDialog.ShowAsync()` when the dialog's `XamlRoot` is sourced from a static
- `AppWindow.GetFromWindowId(...)` chained through `App.MainWindow`
- Any custom title bar / `SetTitleBar` helper that resolves the HWND statically

Safe pattern — assign before activate, defer first navigation:

```csharp
// App.xaml.cs
protected override void OnLaunched(LaunchActivatedEventArgs args)
{
    var window = new MainWindow();
    MainWindow = window;
    window.Activate();
}
```

The `MainWindow` constructor must do only `InitializeComponent` and inert setup — no navigation to a `Page` that reads `App.MainWindow`. Defer the first navigation until after `Activate` completes, either from a `Loaded` handler hooked in `InitializeComponent` or by exposing a `NavigateToInitialPage()` method and calling it from `App.OnLaunched` after the static is assigned.

```csharp
// MainWindow.xaml.cs
public MainWindow()
{
    InitializeComponent();
    this.Activated += (_, _) =>
    {
        if (!_navigated)
        {
            _navigated = true;
            RootFrame.Navigate(typeof(MainPage));
        }
    };
}
private bool _navigated;
```

Validator catches this race with a 10s smoke launch after the build healthcheck passes — see `Validate-UwpMigration.ps1` Section 7.
<a id="background-tasks"></a>
## Background Tasks

`IBackgroundTask` / `BackgroundTaskRegistration` are not the recommended model. Use the WinAppSDK [`BackgroundTaskBuilder`](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/windows-app-sdk/api/winrt/microsoft.windows.applicationmodel.background.backgroundtaskbuilder) (introduced in 1.7), or move the work to push-driven activation / Windows Task Scheduler. See the [background task migration strategy](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/background-task-migration-strategy).

**If you keep the classic out-of-process model** (`Windows.ApplicationModel.Background.BackgroundTaskBuilder` with a string `TaskEntryPoint` such as `"Tasks.SampleBackgroundTask"` + `builder.Register()`) — which is the low-risk way to preserve existing behavior — then two things are **runtime prerequisites**, not optional:

1. The app must be **packaged** (keep `Package.appxmanifest`), and the **build** manifest must declare an `<Extension Category="windows.backgroundTasks" EntryPoint="<Namespace>.<TaskClass>">` for **every** `TaskEntryPoint` string used in code. This declaration lives in the UWP source manifest — carry it into the WinUI 3 project's root `Package.appxmanifest` (see the manifest checklist below). If it is missing, `builder.Register()` **throws at runtime** (`0x80070032`/class-not-registered) even though the app builds and launches fine.

   ```xml
   <Application ...>
     <Extensions>
       <Extension Category="windows.backgroundTasks" EntryPoint="Tasks.SampleBackgroundTask">
         <BackgroundTasks>
           <Task Type="systemEvent" />
           <Task Type="timer" />
         </BackgroundTasks>
       </Extension>
     </Extensions>
   </Application>
   ```

2. Wrap `Register()` in `try/catch` and surface the failure in the UI (e.g. the Status text). The SDK samples call `Register()` with no guard, so a missing-declaration failure otherwise presents as a **silent dead control** (the button click does nothing, Status never changes) — the hardest kind of regression to notice because there is no build error and no crash.
<a id="notifications"></a>
## Notifications

| UWP | WinUI 3 / WinAppSDK |
|-----|---------------------|
| `ToastNotificationManager` (Windows.UI.Notifications) | `AppNotificationManager` (Microsoft.Windows.AppNotifications) |
| WNS push via `PushNotificationChannelManager` | `PushNotificationManager` (Microsoft.Windows.PushNotifications) |

See the [toast notifications guide](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/toast-notifications) and [push notifications guide](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/notifications).

37.         /// <summary>
38.         /// Look up a background task by name.
39.         /// If found, then also report whether the user has granted background execution access.
40.         /// </summary>
41.         public static IBackgroundTaskRegistration LookupBackgroundTask(string name)
42.         {
43.             IBackgroundTaskRegistration found = null;
44. 
45.             // Loop through all background tasks to find one with the matching name
46.             foreach (var entry in BackgroundTaskRegistration.AllTasks)
47.             {
48.                 var task = entry.Value;
49.                 if (task.Name == name)
50.                 {
51.                     found = task;
52.                     break;
53.                 }
54.             }
55. 
56.             if (found != null)
57.             {
58.                 // Check whether the user has already granted background execution access.
59.                 BackgroundAccessStatus backgroundAccessStatus = BackgroundExecutionManager.GetAccessStatus();
60. 
61.                 MainPage rootPage = MainPage.Current;
62.                 switch (backgroundAccessStatus)
63.                 {
64.                     case BackgroundAccessStatus.AlwaysAllowed:
65.                     case BackgroundAccessStatus.AllowedSubjectToSystemPolicy:
66.                         rootPage.NotifyUser("Background task is already registered. Waiting for next update...", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
67.                         break;
68. 
69.                     default:
70.                         rootPage.NotifyUser("Background tasks may be disabled for this app", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
71.                         break;
72.                 }
73.             }
74. 
75.             return found;
76. 
77.         }
78. 
79.         /// <summary>
80.         /// Check and report whether the user has granted background execution access.
81.         /// If so, then also request location access so that the background task can do its work.
82.         /// </summary>
83.         public static async void CheckBackgroundAndRequestLocationAccess()
84.         {
85.             // Get permission for a background task from the user. If the user has already answered once,
86.             // this does nothing and the user must manually update their preference via Settings.
87.             BackgroundAccessStatus backgroundAccessStatus = await BackgroundExecutionManager.RequestAccessAsync();
88. 
89.             MainPage rootPage = MainPage.Current;
90. 
91.             switch (backgroundAccessStatus)
92.             {
93.                 case BackgroundAccessStatus.AlwaysAllowed:
94.                 case BackgroundAccessStatus.AllowedSubjectToSystemPolicy:
95.                     // BackgroundTask is allowed
96.                     rootPage.NotifyUser("Background task registered.", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
97. 
98.                     // Need to request access to location.
99.                     // This must be done when we create the background task registration
100.                     // because the background task cannot display UI.
101.                     // If the user has already answered once,
102.                     // this does nothing and the user must manually update their preference via Settings.
103.                     switch (await Geolocator.RequestAccessAsync())
104.                     {
105.                         case GeolocationAccessStatus.Allowed:
106.                             break;
107. 
108.                         case GeolocationAccessStatus.Denied:
109.                             rootPage.NotifyUser("Access to location is denied.", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
110.                             break;
111. 
112.                         case GeolocationAccessStatus.Unspecified:
113.                             rootPage.NotifyUser("Unspecified error!", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
114.                             break;
115.                     }
116.                     break;
117. 
118.                 default:
119.                     rootPage.NotifyUser("Background tasks may be disabled for this app", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
120.                     break;
121.             }
122.         }
123. 
124.         /// <summary>
125.         /// Look up a string in the saved LocalSettings, or a default value if no string is found.
126.         /// </summary>
127.         /// <param name="key">The key to look up</param>
128.         /// <param name="defaultValue">The fallback value to use if the key is not found or the value is not a string</param>
129.         /// <returns></returns>
130.         public static string LookupSavedString(string key, string defaultValue = "")
131.         {
132.             var values = Windows.Storage.ApplicationData.Current.LocalSettings.Values;
133.             object o;
134.             values.TryGetValue(key, out o);
135.             string s = o as string;
136.             return (s == null) ? defaultValue : s;
137.         }
138. 
139.         /// <summary>
140.         /// Look up a JSON string in the saved LocalSettings and fill the ItemsControl with the strings in it.
141.         /// </summary>
142.         /// <param name="control"></param>
143.         /// <param name="key"></param>
144.         public static void FillItemsFromSavedJson(ItemsControl control, string key)
145.         {
146.             string json = LookupSavedString(key);
147.             if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(json))
148.             {
149.                 // remove all entries and repopulate
150.                 var items = control.Items;
151.                 items.Clear();
152. 
153.                 foreach (IJsonValue element in JsonValue.Parse(json).GetArray())
154.                 {
155.                     items.Add(element.GetString());
156.                 }
157.             }
158.         }
159. 
160.         /// <summary>
161.         /// Look up the "Status" in the saved LocalSettings and display it if present.
162.         /// </summary>
163.         public static void ReportSavedStatus()
164.         {
165.             string status = LookupSavedString("Status");
166.             if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(status))
167.             {
168.                 MainPage.Current.NotifyUser(status, NotifyType.StatusMessage);
169.             }
170.         }
171.     }

37.         /// <summary>
38.         /// Look up a background task by name.
39.         /// If found, then also report whether the user has granted background execution access.
40.         /// </summary>
41.         public static IBackgroundTaskRegistration LookupBackgroundTask(string name)
42.         {
43.             IBackgroundTaskRegistration found = null;
44. 
45.             // Loop through all background tasks to find one with the matching name
46.             foreach (var entry in BackgroundTaskRegistration.AllTasks)
47.             {
48.                 var task = entry.Value;
49.                 if (task.Name == name)
50.                 {
51.                     found = task;
52.                     break;
53.                 }
54.             }
55. 
56.             if (found != null)
57.             {
58.                 // Check whether the user has already granted background execution access.
59.                 BackgroundAccessStatus backgroundAccessStatus = BackgroundExecutionManager.GetAccessStatus();
60. 
61.                 MainPage rootPage = MainPage.Current;
62.                 switch (backgroundAccessStatus)
63.                 {
64.                     case BackgroundAccessStatus.AlwaysAllowed:
65.                     case BackgroundAccessStatus.AllowedSubjectToSystemPolicy:
66.                         rootPage.NotifyUser("Background task is already registered. Waiting for next update...", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
67.                         break;
68. 
69.                     default:
70.                         rootPage.NotifyUser("Background tasks may be disabled for this app", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
71.                         break;
72.                 }
73.             }
74. 
75.             return found;
76. 
77.         }
78. 
79.         /// <summary>
80.         /// Check and report whether the user has granted background execution access.
81.         /// If so, then also request location access so that the background task can do its work.
82.         /// </summary>
83.         public static async void CheckBackgroundAndRequestLocationAccess()
84.         {
85.             // Get permission for a background task from the user. If the user has already answered once,
86.             // this does nothing and the user must manually update their preference via Settings.
87.             BackgroundAccessStatus backgroundAccessStatus = await BackgroundExecutionManager.RequestAccessAsync();
88. 
89.             MainPage rootPage = MainPage.Current;
90. 
91.             switch (backgroundAccessStatus)
92.             {
93.                 case BackgroundAccessStatus.AlwaysAllowed:
94.                 case BackgroundAccessStatus.AllowedSubjectToSystemPolicy:
95.                     // BackgroundTask is allowed
96.                     rootPage.NotifyUser("Background task registered.", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
97. 
98.                     // Need to request access to location.
99.                     // This must be done when we create the background task registration
100.                     // because the background task cannot display UI.
101.                     // If the user has already answered once,
102.                     // this does nothing and the user must manually update their preference via Settings.
103.                     switch (await Geolocator.RequestAccessAsync())
104.                     {
105.                         case GeolocationAccessStatus.Allowed:
106.                             break;
107. 
108.                         case GeolocationAccessStatus.Denied:
109.                             rootPage.NotifyUser("Access to location is denied.", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
110.                             break;
111. 
112.                         case GeolocationAccessStatus.Unspecified:
113.                             rootPage.NotifyUser("Unspecified error!", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
114.                             break;
115.                     }
116.                     break;
117. 
118.                 default:
119.                     rootPage.NotifyUser("Background tasks may be disabled for this app", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
120.                     break;
121.             }
122.         }
123. 
124.         /// <summary>
125.         /// Look up a string in the saved LocalSettings, or a default value if no string is found.
126.         /// </summary>
127.         /// <param name="key">The key to look up</param>
128.         /// <param name="defaultValue">The fallback value to use if the key is not found or the value is not a string</param>
129.         /// <returns></returns>
130.         public static string LookupSavedString(string key, string defaultValue = "")
131.         {
132.             var values = Windows.Storage.ApplicationData.Current.LocalSettings.Values;
133.             object o;
134.             values.TryGetValue(key, out o);
135.             string s = o as string;
136.             return (s == null) ? defaultValue : s;
137.         }
138. 
139.         /// <summary>
140.         /// Look up a JSON string in the saved LocalSettings and fill the ItemsControl with the strings in it.
141.         /// </summary>
142.         /// <param name="control"></param>
143.         /// <param name="key"></param>
144.         public static void FillItemsFromSavedJson(ItemsControl control, string key)
145.         {
146.             string json = LookupSavedString(key);
147.             if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(json))
148.             {
149.                 // remove all entries and repopulate
150.                 var items = control.Items;
151.                 items.Clear();
152. 
153.                 foreach (IJsonValue element in JsonValue.Parse(json).GetArray())
154.                 {
155.                     items.Add(element.GetString());
156.                 }
157.             }
158.         }
159. 
160.         /// <summary>
161.         /// Look up the "Status" in the saved LocalSettings and display it if present.
162.         /// </summary>
163.         public static void ReportSavedStatus()
164.         {
165.             string status = LookupSavedString("Status");
166.             if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(status))
167.             {
168.                 MainPage.Current.NotifyUser(status, NotifyType.StatusMessage);
169.             }

LookupBackgroundTask(string name)\n+    {\n+        IBackgroundTaskRegistration? found = null;\n+\n+        foreach (var entry in BackgroundTaskRegistration.AllTasks)\n+        {\n+            var task = entry.Value;\n+            if (task.Name == name)\n+            {\n+                found = task;\n+                break;\n+            }\n+        }\n+\n+        if (found != null)\n+        {\n+            var backgroundAccessStatus = BackgroundExecutionManager.GetAccessStatus();\n+            var rootPage = Current;\n+            switch (backgroundAccessStatus)\n+            {\n+                case BackgroundAccessStatus.AlwaysAllowed:\n+                case BackgroundAccessStatus.AllowedSubjectToSystemPolicy:\n+                    rootPage.NotifyUser(\"Background task is already registered. defaultValue;\n+    }\n+\n+    public static void FillItemsFromSavedJson(ItemsControl control, string key)\n+    {\n+        var json = LookupSavedString(key);\n+        if (string.IsNullOrEmpty(json))\n+        {\n+            return;\n+        }\n+\n+        var items = control.Items;\n+        items.Clear();\n+\n+        foreach (var element in JsonValue.Parse(json).GetArray())\n+        {\n+            items.Add(element.GetString());\n+        }\n+    }\n+\n+    public static void ReportSavedStatus()\n+    {\n+        var status = LookupSavedString(\"Status\");\n+        if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(status))\n+        {\n+            Current.NotifyUser(status, NotifyType.StatusMessage);\n+        }\n+    }\n+\n+    public void NotifyUser(string message, NotifyType type)\n+    {\n+        StatusBlock.Text = message;\n+        StatusBorder.Background = type == NotifyType.ErrorMessage\n+            ?
